# THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA (HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA).

<meta>Institutional stamps from the Government of India and the Central Secretariat Library are visible at the top corners of the original page.</meta>

->Entering the opening, it then travels within the Channel of Brahma.<-
->Therefore, the Root Lock should always be performed by Yogis. 68.<-
<note>original: "बिलं प्रविश्यैव ततो ब्रह्मनाड्यन्तरं व्रजेत् । तस्मान्नित्यं मूलबंध: कर्तव्यो योगिभि: सदा ॥ ६८ ॥"</note>

It enters the <term>Brahma Nâdî</term> <note>The central spiritual channel of the subtle body, also known as the Susumnâ.</note>, just as a serpent enters its hole. Therefore, the Yogî should always practise this <term>Mûla Bandha</term> (Root Lock). 68.

### THE THROAT LOCK (JÂLANDHARA BANDHA).

->Contracting the throat, one should place the chin firmly on the chest.<-
->This lock is called Jâlandhara; it is the destroyer of old age and death. 69.<-
<note>original: "कंठमाकुंच्य हृदये स्थापयेच्चिबुकं दृढम् । बंधो जालंधराख्योऽयं जरामृत्युविनाशक: ॥ ६९ ॥"</note>

->*The Jâlandhara Bandha.*<-

Contract the throat and press the chin firmly against the chest. This is called <term>Jâlandhara Bandha</term>, which destroys old age and death. 69.

->It binds the network of vessels and the downward-flowing water of the sky.<-
->Therefore, the Jâlandhara Lock destroys a multitude of throat ailments. 70.<-
<note>original: "बध्नाति हि शिराजालमधोगामि नभोजलम् । ततो जालंधरो बंध: कंठदु:खौघनाशन: ॥ ७० ॥"</note>

It stops the opening (hole) of the group of the <term>Nâdîs</term> <note>Subtle energy channels.</note>, through which the juice from the sky (from the <term>Soma</term> or <term>Chandra</term> <note>The "Moon" center in the brain, believed to secrete a divine nectar or "Amrita."</note> in the brain) falls down. It is, therefore, called the <term>Jâlandhara Bandha</term>—the destroyer of a host of diseases of the throat. 70.

->When the Jâlandhara Lock is performed, characterized by the contraction of the throat,<-
->the nectar does not fall into the fire, nor is the air disturbed. 71.<-
<note>original: "जालंधरे कृते बंधे कंठसंकोचलक्षणे । न पीयूषं पतत्यग्नौ न च वायु: प्रकुप्यति ॥ ७१ ॥"</note>

In <term>Jâlandhara Bandha</term>, the indications of a perfect contraction of throat are, that the nectar does not fall into the fire (the <term>Sûrya</term> <note>The "Sun" center situated in the navel, which normally "burns" or consumes the life-extending nectar.</note> situated in the navel), and the air is not disturbed. 71.

->By the contraction of the throat alone, one should firmly block the two channels.<-
->This is known as the middle circuit, which binds the sixteen vital parts. 72.<-
<note>original: "कंठसंकोचनेनैव द्वे नाड्यौ स्तंभयेद्दृढम् । मध्यचक्रमिदं ज्ञेयं षोडशाधारबंधनम् ॥ ७२ ॥"</note>

The two <term>Nâdîs</term> <note>Referring to the side channels, Idâ and Pingalâ.</note> should be stopped firmly by contracting the throat. This is called the middle circuit or centre (<term>Madhya Chakra</term>), and it stops the 16 <term>âdhâras</term> (*i.e.*, vital parts). 72.

Note.— ->The thumbs, ankles, knees, thighs, perineum, genitals, and navel;<-
->The heart, neck, throat, and the soft palate;<-
->The nose, the middle of the eyebrows, the forehead, the head, and the opening of Brahma.<-
->These sixteen vital parts are described by the best of Yogis.<-
<note>original: "अंगुष्ठ गुल्फ जानूरु सीवनी लिंगनाभय: । हृद्ग्रीवा कंठदेशश्च लंबिका नासिका तथा ॥ भ्रूमध्यं च ललाटं च मूर्धाच ब्रह्मरंध्रकम् । एतेहि षोडशाधारा: कथिता योगिपुंगवै: ॥"</note>

The sixteen vital parts mentioned by renowned Yogîs are the (1) thumbs, (2) ankles, (3) knees, (4) thighs, (5) the perineum, (6) organs of generation, (7) the navel, (8) the heart, (9) the neck, (10) the throat, (11) the palate, (12) the nose, (13) the middle of the eyebrows, (14) the forehead, (15) the head and (16) the <term>Brahma randhra</term> <note>The "aperture of Brahma" at the crown of the head.</note>.

->Contracting the root location, one should then perform the Upward Lock.<-
->By binding the Idâ and Pingalâ, one should make the breath flow through the back path. 73.<-
<note>original: "मूलस्थानं समाकुंच्य उड्डीयानं तु कारयेत् ॥ इडां च पिंगलां बध्वा वाहयेत्पश्चिमे पथि ॥ ७३ ॥"</note>

By drawing up the <term>mûlasthâna</term> (the perineum or anus area), <term>Uddîyâna Bandha</term> <note>The Abdominal Lock.</note> should be performed. The flow of the air should be directed to the <term>Susumnâ</term> <note>The "back path" or central spinal channel.</note>, by closing the <term>Idâ</term> and the <term>Pingalâ</term> <note>The left and right energy channels.</note>. 73.

<summary>This page describes the technical execution and benefits of Jâlandhara Bandha (the Throat Lock), which prevents the divine nectar of the "Moon" from being consumed by the "Sun" in the navel. It also lists the sixteen vital points (âdhâras) of the body affected by these practices.</summary>
<keywords>Jâlandhara Bandha, Throat Lock, Mûla Bandha, Nâdî, Soma, Sûrya, Amrita, 16 âdhâras, Brahma randhra, Susumnâ</keywords>

# THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA (HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA).

<meta>Institutional stamps from the Government of India and the Central Secretariat Library are visible at the top corners of the original page.</meta>

->Through this very method, the breath reaches a state of stillness.<-
->Therefore, death, old age, disease, and the like do not arise. 74.<-
<note>original: "अनेनैव विधानेन प्रयाति पवनो लयम् । ततो न जायते मृत्युर्जरारोगादिकं तथा ॥ ७४ ॥"</note>

By this practice, the <term>Prâna</term> <note>The vital life-force or breath.</note> becomes calm and latent. Consequently, the practitioner is freed from the threat of death, the decay of old age, and the onset of disease. 74.

->These three locks are the best of all and are practiced by the great perfected masters.<-
->Yogis know them as the primary means for all Hatha Yoga practices. 75.<-
<note>original: "बंधत्रयमिदं श्रेष्ठं महासिद्धैश्च सेवितम् । सर्वेषां हठतंत्राणां साधनं योगिनो विदुः ॥ ७५ ॥"</note>

These three <term>Bandhas</term> <note>Internal energy "locks": Mûla Bandha (root), Uddiyâna Bandha (abdominal), and Jâlandhara Bandha (throat).</note> are considered superior and have been traditionally practiced by the <term>Mahasiddhas</term> <note>Great "perfected ones" or masters of yoga.</note>. Among all the techniques of <term>Haṭha Yoga</term>, Yogis recognize these as the most essential for achieving success. 75.

->Whatever divine nectar flows from the Moon is swallowed by the Sun;<-
->Because of this, the body is overtaken by old age. 76.<-
<note>original: "यत्किंचित्स्रवते चंद्रादमृतं दिव्यरूपिणः । तत्सर्वं ग्रसते सूर्यस्तेन पिंडो जरायुतः ॥ ७६ ॥"</note>

Every drop of the divine nectar that exudes from the <term>Chandra</term> <note>The "Moon" center, located in the head, thought to produce a cooling vital essence.</note> is consumed by the <term>Sûrya</term> <note>The "Sun" center, located in the navel/solar plexus, associated with heat and digestion.</note>; it is due to this internal "burning" of essence that the physical body eventually withers and grows old. 76.

->There is a divine practice to deceive the mouth of the Sun.<-
->It is learned from the teacher's instruction, not through millions of scripture studies. 77.<-
<note>original: "तत्रास्ति करणं दिव्यं सूर्यस्य मुखवंचनम् । गुरूपदेशतो ज्ञेयं न तु शास्त्रार्थकोटिभिः ॥ ७७ ॥"</note>

To prevent this loss of essence, there exists an excellent technique to bypass or "deceive" the opening of the Sun. This secret method can only be truly understood through the personal guidance of a <term>Guru</term> <note>Spiritual teacher.</note>, and cannot be mastered even by studying the logic of the scriptures for a lifetime. 77.

### THE INVERTED ACTION (VIPARITA KARANĪ).

->The Sun is above the navel and the Moon is below the palate.<-
->The "Inverted Action" is learned through the words of the teacher. 78.<-
<note>original: "ऊर्ध्वं नाभेराधस्तालोरूर्ध्वं भानुरधः शशी । करणी विपरीताख्या गुरुवाक्येन लभ्यते ॥ ७८ ॥"</note>

->*The Viparita Karani.*<-

In the normal state, the Sun <note>The heating element.</note> is located near the navel and the Moon <note>The cooling element.</note> is below the palate. The practice known as <term>Viparita Karani</term> <note>Literally "Inverted Action," often practiced as a headstand or supported shoulder stand.</note>, which reverses these positions, is obtained only through the teacher's instructions. 78.

->For one who practices daily, the digestive fire is increased.<-
->Therefore, the practitioner should ensure an abundance of food.<-
->If the food is insufficient, the fire will consume the body instantly. 79.<-
<note>original: "नित्यमभ्यासयुक्तस्य जठराग्निविवर्धिनी । आहारो बहुलस्तस्य संपाद्यः साधकस्य च ॥ अल्पाहारो यदि भवेद्ग्निर्दहति तत्क्षणात् ॥ ७९ ॥"</note>

This specific exercise greatly stimulates the <term>Jatharâgni</term> <note>The internal digestive fire.</note>. Consequently, anyone practicing this must ensure they consume plenty of food. If the practitioner eats too little, the intensified internal fire will begin to burn away the body's own tissues. 79.

->One should stand with the head down and feet up for a moment on the first day.<-
->Increase this duration slightly day by day. 80.<-
<note>original: "अधःशिराश्चोर्ध्वपादः क्षणं स्यात्प्रथमे दिने ॥ क्षणाच्च किंचिदधिकमभ्यसेच्च दिने दिने ॥ ८० ॥"</note>

Place the head on the ground and the feet upward toward the sky. On the first day, remain in this position for only a moment, and then gradually increase the duration of the practice every day. 80.

<summary>This page concludes the benefits of the three major Bandhas (locks) and introduces the Viparita Karani (Inverted Action). It explains how inversions prevent the "Sun" in the navel from consuming the "Nectar of the Moon," thereby halting aging, while warning that such practices require increased food intake to fuel the stimulated digestive fire.</summary>
<keywords>Viparita Karani, Sun and Moon energy, Jatharagni, digestive fire, immortality nectar, Bandhas, Hatha Yoga, Guru-disciple tradition</keywords>

<meta>This page continues from the discussion on the Inverted Action (Viparita Karani) mudra, which involves balancing the lunar and solar energies within the body. # THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA (HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA).

->Wrinkles and grey hair are no longer seen after six months of practice.<-
->He who practices this daily, even for just three hours, conquers time itself. 81.<-
<note>original: "वलितं पलितं चैव षण्मासोर्ध्वं न दृश्यते ॥ याममात्रं तु यो नित्यमभ्यसेत्स तु कालजित् ॥ ८१ ॥"</note>

After six months of dedicated practice, the physical signs of aging—such as wrinkles and grey hair—disappear. The practitioner who devotes himself to this daily, for a period of one <term>Yama</term> <note>A traditional unit of time lasting approximately three hours.</note>, transcends the limitations of time and death. 81.

### The Practice of Vajroli

->Even one who lives as they please, without following the rules of Yoga,<-
->Becomes a vessel for success if they master Vajroli. 82.<-
<note>original: "स्वेच्छया वर्तमानोऽपि योगोक्तैर्नियमैर्विना । वज्रोलीं यो विजानाति स योगी सिद्धिभाजनम् ॥ ८२ ॥"</note>

->*The Vajroli.*<-

Even a person who lives a conventional or wayward life, without strictly adhering to the rigorous disciplines or dietary rules usually required in Yoga, can achieve spiritual perfection if they understand and master the practice of <term>Vajroli</term> <note>A practice involving the control of the urogenital muscles to conserve vital energy.</note>. Such a person is a true Yogi and is destined for success. 82.

->I shall now describe two things which are difficult for anyone to obtain:<-
->The first is milk, and the second is a cooperative woman. 83.<-
<note>original: "तत्रवस्तुद्वयं वक्ष्ये दुर्लभं यस्य कस्य चित् । क्षीरं चैकं द्वितीयं तु नारी च वशवर्तिनी ॥ ८३ ॥"</note>

There are two essential requirements for this practice that are often difficult for ordinary people to find: (1) a steady supply of pure milk and (2) a female partner who is likewise trained or cooperative in the practice. 83.

->One should practice the gradual and thorough upward contraction of the sexual organ.<-
->Whether one is a man or a woman, one can attain success in Vajroli. 84.<-
<note>original: "मेहनन शनैः सम्यगूर्ध्वाकुञ्चनमभ्यसेत् ॥ पुरुषोऽप्यथवा नारी वज्रोलीसिद्धिमाप्नुयात् ॥ ८४ ॥"</note>

By practicing the slow and controlled upward contraction of the <term>mehana</term> <note>The urethra or sexual organ.</note>, the practitioner learns to draw energy upward. This technique is not limited by gender; both men and women can achieve the "perfection" or <term>Siddhi</term> of Vajroli through this method. 84.

->With effort, using a proper tube, one should blow air into the passage.<-
->This should be done very slowly to facilitate the movement of air. 85.<-
<note>original: "यत्नतः शस्तनालेन फूत्कारं वज्रकंदरे । शनैः शनैः प्रकुर्वीत वायुसंचारकारणात् ॥ ८५ ॥"</note>

Using a specially prepared tube, the practitioner should carefully and slowly blow air into the <term>Vajra-kandara</term> <note>Literally the "diamond cave," referring to the urethral canal.</note>. This preparation helps clear the passage and control the internal movement of air and vital force. 85.

->Through practice, the vital essence falling into the female organ is drawn upward.<-
->One should draw back and preserve one's own vital essence, even if it has been discharged. 86.<-
<note>original: "नारीभगे पतद्बिन्दुमभ्यासेनोर्ध्वमाहरेत् । चलितं च निजं बिन्दुमुर्ध्वमाकृष्य रक्षयेत् ॥ ८६ ॥"</note>

Through specific training, the <term>bindu</term> <note>The vital essence or seed, believed to hold the potential for immortality.</note> can be drawn back upward. Even if the essence has begun to move or be discharged, the Yogi can reclaim it and preserve it within their own body. 86.

->The knower of Yoga who preserves the vital essence in this way overcomes death.<-
->Death comes from the loss of essence; life is sustained by its preservation. 87.<-
<note>original: "एवं संरक्षयेद् बिन्दुं मृत्युं जयति योगवित् । मरणं बिन्दुपातेन जीवनं बिन्दुधारणात् ॥ ८७ ॥"</note>

The practitioner who masters the art of preserving their <term>bindu</term> conquers death. In the philosophy of Hatha Yoga, the loss of this vital essence leads to physical decay and eventually death, whereas its internal retention and sublimation lead to longevity and vitality. 87.

->A pleasant fragrance arises in the Yogi's body from the preservation of the essence.<-
->As long as the essence remains steady in the body, why should there be any fear of death? 88.<-
<note>original: "सुगंधो योगिनो देहे जायते बिंदुधारणात् । यावद् बिंदुः स्थिरो देहे तावत्कालभयं कुतः ॥ ८८ ॥"</note>

<meta>Catchword at bottom left: G</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the discussion on the age-reversing benefits of Inverted Action and introduces Vajroli Mudra, a practice focused on the preservation and upward movement of vital fluids (bindu) to achieve immortality and physical health.</summary>
<keywords>Vajroli Mudra, Bindu, vital essence, longevity, Hatha Yoga Pradipika, internal alchemy, overcoming death</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion on the Vajroli Mudra and its variations, focusing on the conservation of vital energy (bindu) and its role in longevity and spiritual liberation. It moves from the general benefits of energy preservation to specific secondary practices known as Sahajoli and Amaroli.</meta>

# THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA (HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA).

By preserving <term>bindu</term> <note>Literally "a drop"; in Hatha Yoga, it refers to the vital essence or life-force, often associated with reproductive fluids, which is believed to grant longevity when conserved.</note>, the body of the Yogi emits a pleasing fragrance. As long as this vital essence is firmly established within the body, there is no fear of death. 88.

->The vital fluid of men is governed by the mind, and life itself depends upon that fluid.<-
->Therefore, both the mind and the vital fluid should be protected with great effort. 89.<-
<note>original: "चित्तायत्तं नृणां शुक्रं शुक्रायत्तं च जीवितम् । तस्माच्छुक्रं मनश्चैव रक्षणीयं प्रयत्नतः ॥ ८९ ॥"</note>

The vital essence in humans is under the control of the mind, and the span of one's life is dependent on this essence. Consequently, the practitioner must strive to guard both their mental focus and their physical vitality by all possible means. 89.

### The Practice of Sahajoli

->Sahajoli and Amaroli are variations of the Vajroli practice.<-
->One should take the ashes of burnt cow dung and mix them with water. 90.<-
<note>original: "सहजोलिचामरोलिर्वज्रोल्या भेद एकतः । जलेषु भस्म निक्षिप्य दग्धगोमयसंभवम् ॥ ९० ॥"</note>

->*The Sahajoli.*<-

<term>Sahajoli</term> and <term>Amaroli</term> are considered different forms or stages of the primary <term>Vajroli</term> practice. To perform this, ashes produced from burning dried cow dung <note>A substance considered purifying in many traditional Indian rituals.</note> should be mixed with water. 90.

->After the physical practice of Vajroli, the man and woman, sitting comfortably,<-
->Should rub this mixture onto their own bodies. 91.<-
<note>original: "वज्रोलीमैथुनादूर्ध्वं स्त्रीपुंसोः स्वांगलेपनम् ॥ आसीनयोः सुखेनैव मुक्तव्यापयोः क्षणात् ॥ ९१ ॥"</note>

Once the physical exercises of Vajroli are completed, both the male and female practitioners should sit at ease and apply the prepared ash-water to their limbs. 91.

->This is known as Sahajoli, and it should always be trusted by Yogis.<-
->This auspicious practice brings about spiritual liberation even for those enjoying worldly life. 92.<-
<note>original: "सहजोलिरियं प्रोक्ता श्रद्धेया योगिभिः सदा ॥ अयं शुभकरो योगो भोगयुक्तोऽपि मुक्तिदः ॥ ९२ ॥"</note>

This method is called Sahajoli, and it is a practice that Yogis should rely upon. This beneficial Yoga is so powerful that it leads to <term>moksha</term> <note>Ultimate spiritual liberation or freedom from the cycle of rebirth.</note>, even for those who continue to experience physical pleasures or a householder's life. 92.

->This Yoga is attained by virtuous, steady men who see the truth of reality;<-
->It is mastered by those free from envy, and not by those who are jealous. 93.<-
<note>original: "अयं योगः पुण्यवतां धीराणां तत्त्वदर्शिनाम् ॥ निर्मत्सराणां सिद्ध्येत न तु मत्सरशालिनाम् ॥ ९३ ॥"</note>

This practice is successful for those who are courageous, virtuous, and possess a clear perception of reality. It can only be mastered by those who have purified themselves of envy and jealousy, and cannot be accomplished by those who remain caught in such negative emotions. 93.

### The Practice of Amaroli

->Discarding the first flow of water because it has too much bile, and the final flow because it is worthless;<-
->The cool middle flow is consumed in the Amaroli practice, according to the doctrine of the Kapalikas. 94.<-
<note>original: "पित्तोल्बणत्त्वात्प्रथमांबुधारां विहाय निःसारतयांत्यधारा । निषेव्यते शीतलमध्यधारा कापालिके खंडमतेऽमरोली ॥ ९४ ॥"</note>

->*The Amaroli.*<-

According to the teachings of the <term>Kapalika</term> <note>An ancient, ascetic sect of Shiva-worshippers known for their unconventional and esoteric rituals.</note> sect, the practice of <term>Amaroli</term> involves the consumption of the "middle stream" of urine. The first part of the flow is discarded because it is too acidic (containing "excess bile"), and the final part is ignored because it lacks potency. The cool, middle portion is what is utilized. 94.

<vocab>bindu, Sahajolî, Amarolî, Vajrolî, mokṣa, Kâpâlikas, Yoga</vocab>

<summary>This page describes the esoteric practices of Sahajoli and Amaroli, which are presented as advanced variations of the Vajroli Mudra. These rituals focus on the preservation of vital essence (bindu) and physical purification to achieve spiritual liberation (moksha), even for those living worldly lives.</summary>
<keywords>Sahajoli, Amaroli, Vajroli, bindu conservation, Kapalika sect, spiritual liberation, vital fluid, mental control, ritual ashes</keywords>

# THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA (HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA).

->One who drinks the "immortal fluid" daily, draws it into the nose,<-
->And practices the <term>Vajroli</term> <note>A practice involving the control of the lower physical centers to conserve vital energy.</note> method correctly, is said to be practicing <term>Amaroli</term>. 95.<-
<note>original: "अमरीं यः पिबेन्नित्यं नस्यं कुर्वन् दिने दिने ॥ वज्रोलीभभ्यसेत्सम्यगमरोलीति कथ्यते ॥ ९५ ॥"</note>

One who consumes the <term>Amari</term> <note>Literally "the immortal"; a term used for the practitioner's own urine in this specific esoteric context, believed to have medicinal and spiritual properties.</note> every day, performs nasal cleansing with it, and masters the Vajroli technique, is engaged in the practice known as Amaroli. 95.

->The lunar essence discharged during the practice should be mixed with ashes;<-
->By applying it to the upper parts of the body, divine vision is born. 96.<-
<note>original: "अभ्यासान्निःसृता चांद्रीं विभूत्या सह मिश्रयेत् ॥ धारयेदुत्तमांगेषु दिव्यदृष्टिः प्रजायते ॥ ९६ ॥"</note>

The <term>bindu</term> <note>The vital seed or life-force.</note>—associated with the cool, lunar energy of the head—that is released during Vajroli should be blended with sacred ashes. Rubbing this mixture onto the forehead and upper limbs grants the practitioner "divine sight" or clairvoyance. 96.

### The Stirring of the Power (Shakti Chalana)

->Crooked-bodied, the Coiled One, the She-Serpent, Power, the Sovereign Goddess,<-
->The Coiled, the Unobstructed—these words are all synonymous. 97.<-
<note>original: "कुटिलांगी कुण्डलिनी भुजंगी शक्तिरीश्वरी । कुण्डल्यरुन्धती चैते शब्दाः पर्यायवाचकाः ॥ ९७ ॥"</note>

->*The Stirring of the Power.*<-

<term>Kutilangi</term> (crooked-bodied), <term>Kundalini</term> (the coiled one), <term>Bhujangi</term> (a female serpent), <term>Shakti</term> (primordial power), <term>Ishwari</term> (the sovereign goddess), <term>Kundali</term>, and <term>Arundhati</term> (the morning star/unobstructed)—all these terms refer to the same latent spiritual energy within the body. 97.

->Just as a door is opened by force with a key,<-
->So the Yogi should break open the door to liberation by means of Kundalini. 98.<-
<note>original: "उद्घाटयेत्कपाटं तु यथा कुञ्चिकया हठात् ॥ कुण्डलिन्या तथा योगी मोक्षद्वारं विभेदयेत् ॥ ९८ ॥"</note>

In the same way that a key is used to forcibly unlock a door, the practitioner of <term>Hatha Yoga</term> uses the awakening of the Kundalini energy to throw open the gates to spiritual freedom. 98.

->By the path one must travel to reach the painless seat of the Creator,<-
->The Supreme Goddess sleeps, covering that door with her mouth. 99.<-
<note>original: "येन मार्गेण गंतव्यं ब्रह्मस्थानं निरामयम् ॥ मुखेनाच्छाद्य तद्द्वारं प्रसुप्ता परमेश्वरी ॥ ९९ ॥"</note>

The <term>Parameshwari</term> (the Supreme Goddess, Kundalini) remains in a state of sleep, blocking with her own mouth the entrance to the <term>Sushumna</term> <note>The central energy channel of the spine.</note>—the path that leads to the realm of <term>Brahma</term>, a state of existence entirely free from suffering. 99.

->The Kundalini power sleeps above the bulb, granting liberation to Yogis<-
->And bondage to the ignorant. One who knows her, knows Yoga. 100.<-
<note>original: "कंदोर्ध्वं कुण्डली शक्तिः सुप्ता मोक्षाय योगिनाम् ॥ बंधनाय च मूढानां यस्तां वेत्ति स योगवित् ॥ १०० ॥"</note>

The Kundalini energy rests in a dormant state above the <term>kanda</term> <note>A root-like bulb located at the base of the spine.</note>. For the disciplined Yogi, she is the key to liberation; for the uninitiated, she is the cause of continued worldly attachment. One who understands the nature of this power is a true master of Yoga. 100.

->The Kundalini is described as having a coiled shape, like a serpent.<-
->Whoever causes that Power to move is undoubtedly liberated. 101.<-
<note>original: "कुण्डली कुटिलाकारा सर्पवत्परिकीर्तिता ॥ सा शक्तिश्चालिता येन स मुक्तो नात्र संशयः ॥ १०१ ॥"</note>

The Kundalini is traditionally depicted as being coiled like a snake. There is no doubt that anyone who succeeds in stirring this Power and making it rise is truly <term>Mukta</term> (released from the cycle of rebirth). 101.

->Between the Ganges and the Yamuna sits the young widow ascetic;<-
->By force, one should seize her to reach the supreme state of Vishnu. 102.<-
<note>original: "गंगायामुनयोर्मध्ये बालरंडा तपस्विनी ॥ बलात्कारेण गृह्णीयात्तद्विष्णोः परमं पदम् ॥ १०२ ॥"</note>
<note>The "Ganges" and "Yamuna" are allegories for the Ida and Pingala nerve channels. The "young widow" refers to the Kundalini energy residing in the middle path.</note>

<summary>This page concludes the Amaroli practice and introduces Shaktichalana, the method for awakening Kundalini. It defines Kundalini through various synonyms and emphasizes its role as the "key" to opening the door to spiritual liberation.</summary>

# THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA (HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA)

That young <term>Tapaswini</term> <note>A female ascetic; here a metaphor for the Kundalini energy.</note>, who lies between the Ganges and the Yamuna—symbolizing the <term>Ida</term> and <term>Pingala</term> <note>The left (lunar) and right (solar) energy channels in the subtle body.</note>—should be seized by force to attain the highest state of consciousness. 102.

->The Ida is the goddess Ganges, and the Pingala is the river Yamuna.<-
->Between the Ida and the Pingala resides the infant widow, the <term>Kundalini</term>. 103.<-
<note>original: "इडा भगवती गंगा पिंगला यमुना नदी ॥ इडापिंगलयोर्मध्ये बालरंडा च कुंडली ॥ १०३ ॥"</note>

->One should awaken that sleeping she-serpent by catching hold of her tail.<-
->By the force of <term>Hatha</term> <note>Physical or "forceful" yoga.</note>, that <term>Shakti</term> <note>Divine power or energy.</note> leaves her sleep and rises upward. 104.<-
<note>original: "पुच्छे प्रगृह्य भुजगीं सुप्तामुद्बोधयेच्च ताम् ॥ निद्रां विहाय सा शक्तिरूर्ध्वमुत्तिष्ठते हठात् ॥ १०४ ॥"</note>

->This hooded serpent resides in the <term>Muladhara</term> <note>The root center at the base of the spine.</note>. She should be grasped and moved daily, morning and evening, for half a <term>prahar</term> <note>Approximately 90 minutes; a prahar is a traditional unit of time equal to three hours.</note>,<-
->By filling the body with air through the Sun channel using the <term>Paridhana</term> <note>A method of "wrapping" or directed breathing.</note> technique. 105.<-
<note>original: "अवस्थिता चैव फणावती सा प्रातश्च सायं प्रहरार्धमात्रम् ॥ प्रपूर्य सूर्यात्परिधानयुक्त्या प्रगृह्य नित्यं परिचालनीया ॥ १०५ ॥"</note>

->The bulb is located above the anus, one <term>vitasti</term> <note>A span of the hand, roughly 9 inches.</note> long and four <term>angulas</term> <note>Finger-widths, roughly 3 inches.</note> in width.<-
->It is said to be soft and white, appearing as if it were folded cloth. 106.<-
<note>original: "ऊर्ध्वं वितस्तिमात्रं तु विस्तारं चतुरंगुलम् ॥ मृदुलं धवलं प्रोक्तं वेष्टितांबरलक्षणम् ॥ १०६ ॥"</note>

->While established in <term>Vajrasana</term> <note>The "Thunderbolt" or "Diamond" pose; the text suggests it is functionally similar to Padmasana (Lotus Pose) in this context.</note>, hold the feet firmly with the hands.<-
->In that position, one should press the "bulb" which is located near the ankles. 107.<-
<note>original: "सति वज्रासने पादौ कराभ्यां धारयेद्दृढम् ॥ गुल्फदेशसमीपे च कंदं तत्र प्रपीडयेत् ॥ १०७ ॥"</note>

->The Yogi, remaining in Vajrasana and having stirred the Kundalini,<-
->Should then perform <term>Bhastrika</term> <note>The "Bellows Breath," a rapid, forceful breathing technique.</note> to quickly awaken the Kundalini. 108.<-
<note>original: "वज्रासने स्थितो योगी चालयित्वा च कुंडलीम् ॥ कुर्यादनंतरं भस्त्रां कुंडलीमाशु बोधयेत् ॥ १०८ ॥"</note>

->One should contract the <term>Bhanu</term> <note>Literally "the Sun," referring to the solar center or navel.</note> and move the Kundalini.<-
->Even if one has entered the mouth of Death, what fear of death can there be for him? 109.<-
<note>original: "भानोराकुंचनं कुर्यात्कुंडलीं चालयेत्ततः ॥ मृत्युवक्त्रगतस्यापि तस्य मृत्युभयं कुतः ॥ १०९ ॥"</note>

<summary>This section describes the physical and energetic awakening of the Kundalini serpent residing in the 'bulb' at the base of the spine. It details specific measurements of the energy centers and prescribes a combination of the Vajrasana posture, Bhastrika breath, and navel contraction to rouse the goddess-power.</summary>

# THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA (HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA)

->By moving this energy for two <term>muhurtas</term> <note>A traditional unit of time; two muhurtas equal approximately 96 minutes.</note> without fear, it is drawn upward slightly, entering the <term>Sushumna</term> <note>The central energy channel within the spinal column, associated with spiritual awakening.</note>. 110.<-
<note>original: "मुहूर्तद्वयपर्यन्तं निर्भयं चालनादसौ ॥ ऊर्ध्वमाकृष्यते किंचित्सुषुम्नायां समुद्गता ॥ ११० ॥"</note>

->By this action, <term>Kundalini</term> definitely leaves the entrance of the Sushumna, and the <term>Prana</term> <note>The vital life force or breath.</note> enters it naturally. 111.<-
<note>original: "तेन कुण्डलिनी तस्याः सुषुम्नाया मुखं ध्रुवम् ॥ जहाति तस्मात्प्राणोऽयं सुषुम्नां व्रजति स्वतः ॥ १११ ॥"</note>

->Therefore, one should always move this comfortably sleeping <term>Arundhati</term> <note>Another name for the Kundalini, used here as a poetic personification.</note>; by moving her, the Yogi is liberated from all diseases. 112.<-
<note>original: "तस्मात्संचालयेन्नित्यं सुखसुप्तामरुन्धतीम् ॥ तस्याः संचालनेनैव योगी रोगैः प्रमुच्यते ॥ ११२ ॥"</note>

->The Yogi who has moved this <term>Shakti</term> <note>The divine power or internal energy.</note> becomes a vessel for spiritual success. What more need be said? He conquers death with ease. 113.<-
<note>original: "येन संचालिता शक्तिः स योगी सिद्धिभाजनम् ॥ किमत्र बहुनोक्तेन कालं जयति लीलया ॥ ११३ ॥"</note>

->For one devoted to <term>Brahmacharya</term> <note>A lifestyle of self-restraint or continence.</note> and always eating a wholesome, moderate diet, success appears within a <term>mandala</term> <note>A traditional period of approximately 40 days.</note> through the practice of Kundalini yoga. 114.<-
<note>original: "ब्रह्मचर्यरतस्यैव नित्यं हितमिताशिनः ॥ मण्डलाद्दृश्यते सिद्धिः कुण्डलभ्यासयोगिनः ॥ ११४ ॥"</note>

->After moving the Kundalini, one should especially perform <term>Bhastra</term> <note>Bellows breath, a rapid and forceful breathing technique used to generate internal heat.</note>. How can a practitioner who masters this have any fear of the Lord of Death? 115.<-
<note>original: "कुण्डलीं चालयित्वा तु भस्त्रां कुर्याद्विशेषतः ॥ एवमभ्यसतो नित्यं यमिनो यमभीः कुतः ॥ ११५ ॥"</note>

->For the purification of the 72,000 <term>nadis</term> <note>The subtle channels through which life force flows throughout the body.</note>, what other means of cleansing is there except for the practice of Kundalini? 116.<-
<note>original: "द्वासप्ततिसहस्राणां नाडीनां मलशोधने ॥ कुतः प्रक्षालनोपायः कुण्डलभ्यासनादृते ॥ ११६ ॥"</note>

->This middle channel, the Sushumna, becomes straight for Yogis through the steady practice of <term>asanas</term> (postures), <term>pranayama</term> (breath control), and <term>mudras</term> (seals or gestures). 117.<-
<note>original: "इयं तु मध्यमा नाडी दृढभ्यासेन योगिनाम् । आसनप्राणायाममुद्राभिः सरला भवेत् ॥ ११७ ॥"</note>

->By holding the mind in <term>Samadhi</term> <note>A state of intense concentration or meditative absorption where the individual consciousness merges with the universal.</note> during practice, for those who are ever-watchful, the <term>Rudrani</term> <note>Another name for Kundalini, associated here with the power of the god Rudra (Shiva).</note> or this Mudra bestows auspicious success. 118.<-
<note>original: "अभ्यासे तु विनिद्राणां मनो धृत्वा समाधिना । रुद्राणी वा यदा मुद्रा भद्रां सिद्धिं प्रयच्छति ॥ ११८ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page details the specific outcomes of rousing the Kundalini, emphasizing that the practice purifies the body's 72,000 energy channels and allows the life force (Prana) to enter the central column. It highlights that success requires a disciplined lifestyle of moderate eating and continence, eventually leading to the conquest of disease and death.</summary>

It emphasizes the necessity of Raja Yoga (meditation/stillness) as the goal of these Hatha practices.</meta>

# THE LIGHT ON HATHA YOGA (HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA)

Those whose need for sleep has decreased through practice and whose minds have become calm through <term>samadhi</term> <note>A state of deep meditative absorption where the individual consciousness merges with the universal.</note> attain beneficial accomplishments through the <term>Shambhavi</term> <note>A specific yogic seal involving a fixed internal gaze.</note> and other <term>mudras</term> <note>Physical gestures or "seals" used to control and direct internal energy.</note>. 118.

->Without <term>Raja Yoga</term> <note>The "Royal Yoga" of the mind and meditation. Here, the text suggests that physical techniques are incomplete without the accompanying mental state.</note>, this earth, the night, and the mudras—no matter how wonderful they may be—do not truly shine. 119.<-
<note>original: "राजयोगं विना पृथ्वी राजयोगं विना निशा । राजयोगं विना मुद्रा विचित्रापि न शोभते ॥ ११९ ॥"</note>

<note>Translator's Note: In this verse, "Earth" symbolizes steadiness and calmness. "Night" refers to <term>Kumbhaka</term> (breath retention); just as a kingdom's activities cease at night, the movement of the <term>Prana</term> (vital breath) ceases during retention. Therefore, "night" signifies the absence of motion.</note>

->Every practice relating to the breath should be performed with a concentrated mind. A wise person should not allow their mental activity to wander. 120.<-
<note>original: "मारुतस्य विधिं सर्वं मनोयुक्तं समभ्यसेत् । इतरत्र न कर्तव्या मनोवृत्तिर्मनीषिणा ॥ १२० ॥"</note>

->These ten mudras were described by <term>Adinatha</term> <note>The "First Lord," a title for the god Shiva, who is considered the original source of Hatha Yoga.</note>. Every one of them bestows great accomplishments upon those who practice self-restraint. 121.<-
<note>original: "इति मुद्रा दश प्रोक्ता आदिनाथेन शंभुना । एकैका तासु यमिनां महासिद्धिप्रदायिनी ॥ १२१ ॥"</note>

->The one who provides the traditional instruction of these mudras, handed down through the lineage, is the true <term>Guru</term> and should be regarded as <term>Ishvara</term> <note>The Divine Lord or God.</note> in human form. 122.<-
<note>original: "उपदेशं हि मुद्राणां यो दत्ते सांप्रदायिकम् । स एव श्रीगुरुः स्वामी साक्षादीश्वर एव सः ॥ १२२ ॥"</note>

->By becoming devoted to his words and remaining focused on the practice of the mudras, the practitioner attains the <term>Siddhis</term> <note>Supernatural or perfected powers.</note> such as <term>Anima</term> <note>The power to become as small as an atom.</note>, and even cheats death itself. 123.<-
<note>original: "तस्य वाक्यपरो भूत्वा मुद्राभ्यासे समाहितः । अणिमादिगुणैः सार्धं लभते कालवञ्चनम् ॥ १२३ ॥"</note>

### Thus ends the third chapter of the Hatha Yoga Pradipika, on the Exposition of the Mudras.
<note>original: "इति हठप्रदीपिकायां तृतीयोपदेशः ॥ ३ ॥"</note>

<meta>This page begins the fourth and final chapter of the Hatha Yoga Pradipika, moving from physical breath control and mudras (seals) to the ultimate goal of yoga: Samadhi. The previous page concluded the instructions on the ten mudras and established that physical techniques are merely the foundation for the "Royal Yoga" of meditation.</meta>

### CHAPTER IV.
->On <term>Samâdhi</term><note>A state of deep meditative absorption where the practitioner's mind merges with the object of meditation, leading to spiritual liberation.</note>.<-

# CHAPTER FOUR
<note>original: "चतुर्थोपदेशः"</note>

->Salutation to <term>Shiva</term>, the Guru, who is the essence of <term>Nâda</term>, <term>Bindu</term>, and <term>Kalâ</term> <note>These are technical terms in tantric yoga: "Nada" is the inner sound, "Bindu" is the point of concentrated consciousness, and "Kala" is the creative energy or manifestation.</note>. One who is devoted to Him reaches the stainless state. 1.<-
<note>original: "नमः शिवाय गुरवेनादबिंदुकलात्मने । निरंजनपदं याति नित्यं यत्र परायणः ॥ १ ॥"</note>

->Now I shall describe the supreme sequence of <term>Samâdhi</term>, which destroys death, provides an easy path to happiness, and grants the highest bliss of the <term>Brahman</term> <note>The ultimate, infinite reality in Hindu philosophy.</note>. 2.<-
<note>original: "अथेदानीं प्रवक्ष्यामि समाधिक्रममुत्तमम् ॥ मृत्युघ्नं च सुखोपायं ब्रह्मानंदकरं परम् ॥ २ ॥"</note>

-><term>Râja Yoga</term>, <term>Samâdhi</term>, <term>Unmanî</term>, <term>Manonmanî</term>, Immortality, Dissolution (<term>Laya</term>), Reality (<term>Tattva</term>), the Void and the Non-Void, the Supreme State,<-
->The Mindless State (<term>Amanaska</term>), Non-duality, the Supportless State, the Stainless State, Liberation while living (<term>Jîvanmukti</term>), the Natural State (<term>Sahajâ</term>), and the Fourth State (<term>Turyâ</term>)—all these are synonymous. 3-4.<-
<note>original: "राजयोगसमाधिश्च उन्मनी च मनोन्मनी । अमरत्वं लयस्तत्त्वं शून्याशून्यं परं पदम् ॥ ३ ॥ अमनस्कं तथा द्वैतं निरालंबं निरंजनम् । जीवन्मुक्तिश्च सहजा तुर्या चेत्येकवाचकाः ॥ ४ ॥"</note>

->Just as salt becomes one with water when dissolved in it, the union of the Soul (<term>Âtmâ</term>) and the mind is called <term>Samâdhi</term>. 5.<-
<note>original: "सलिले सैंधवं यद्वत्साम्यं भजति योगतः । तथात्ममनसोरैक्यं समाधिरभिधीयते ॥ ५ ॥"</note>

->When the life-force (<term>Prâna</term>) becomes still and the mind is absorbed, that state of perfect equilibrium is called <term>Samâdhi</term>. 6.<-
<note>original: "यदा संक्षीयते प्राणो मानसं च प्रलीयते । तदा समरसत्वं च समाधिरभिधीयते ॥ ६ ॥"</note>

->This state of equality, the oneness of the individual self and the Supreme Self, where all mental constructs and intentions cease to exist, is called <term>Samâdhi</term>. 7.<-
<note>original: "तत्सभं च द्वयोरैक्यं जीवात्मपरमात्मनोः । प्रनष्टसर्वसंकल्पः समाधिः सोऽभिधीयते ॥ ७ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page introduces Chapter IV of the Hatha Yoga Pradipika, defining Samâdhi as the ultimate union of the individual soul with the universal consciousness. It provides several technical synonyms for this state and uses the metaphor of salt dissolving in water to illustrate the complete merging of the mind and spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Samâdhi, Raja Yoga, Unmanî, Jivanmukti, Prâna, Âtmâ, Brahmananda, Hatha Yoga Pradipika, Shiva, liberation</keywords>

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

->Who can truly know the greatness of <term>Royal Yoga</term><note>original: "Râja Yoga"; the highest path of meditation and spiritual union.</note>? Knowledge, <term>liberation</term><note>original: "mukti"</note>, stability, and <term>supernatural powers</term><note>original: "siddhis"</note> are obtained only through the instructions of a teacher. 8.<-
<note>original: "राजयोगस्य माहात्म्यं को वा जानाति तत्त्वतः । ज्ञानं मुक्तिः स्थितिः सिद्धिर्गुरुवाक्येन लभ्यते ॥ ८ ॥"</note>

->Renouncing the objects of the senses is difficult to achieve; the vision of Reality is difficult to attain; and the <term>natural state</term><note>original: "sahajâvasthâ"; a state of spontaneous, effortless enlightenment or Samadhi.</note> is difficult to reach without the compassion of a true teacher. 9.<-
<note>original: "दुर्लभो विषयत्यागो दुर्लभं तत्त्वदर्शनम् । दुर्लभा सहजावस्था सद्गुरोः करुणां विना ॥ ९ ॥"</note>

->Through various <term>postures</term><note>original: "âsanas"</note>, different <term>breath retentions</term><note>original: "kumbhakas"</note>, and diverse physical practices, when the <term>Great Power</term><note>original: "mahâśakti" or Kundalini, the spiritual energy at the base of the spine.</note> is awakened, the <term>life-force</term><note>original: "prâṇa"</note> dissolves into the <term>Void</term><note>original: "śûnya," referring to the state of absolute stillness in Samadhi.</note>. 10.<-
<note>original: "विविधैरासनैः कुम्भैर्विचित्रैः करणैरपि । प्रबुद्धायां महाशक्तौ प्राणः शून्ये प्रलीयते ॥ १० ॥"</note>

->For the practitioner whose energy has been awakened and who has abandoned all remaining <term>actions</term><note>original: "karma"</note>, the natural state of absorption arises of its own accord. 11.<-
<note>original: "उत्पन्नशक्तिबोधस्य त्यक्तनिःशेषकर्मणः । योगिनः सहजावस्था स्वयमेव प्रजायते ॥ ११ ॥"</note>

->When the life-force flows through the <term>central channel</term><note>original: "suṣumṇâ," the primary energetic path in the spine.</note> and the mind enters the Void, then the knower of Yoga completely uproots the effects of all past actions. 12.<-
<note>original: "सुषुम्णावाहिनि प्राणे शून्ये विशति मानसे । तदा सर्वाणि कर्माणि निर्मूलयति योगवित् ॥ १२ ॥"</note>

->Salutation to you, O Immortal One! Even <term>Death</term><note>original: "kâla," literally "Time."</note> itself—into whose mouth this entire world of moving and unmoving things has fallen—has been conquered by you. 13.<-
<note>original: "अमराय नमस्तुभ्यं सोऽपिकालस्त्वया जितः । पतितं वदने यस्य जगदेतच्चराचरम् ॥ १३ ॥"</note>

->When the mind has attained equanimity and the breath moves into the middle channel, then the practices of <term>Amaroli</term>, <term>Vajroli</term>, and <term>Sahajoli</term><note>Specific tantric yogic techniques involving the conservation and sublimation of vital fluids and energy.</note> are perfected. 14.<-
<note>original: "चित्ते समत्वमापन्ने वायौ व्रजति मध्यमे । तदामरोली वज्रोली सहजोली प्रजायते ॥ १४ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page explains that the highest state of Yoga (Samadhi) is only possible through a guru's grace and the awakening of Kundalini energy. It describes the physical signs of success, such as the breath entering the central channel and the resulting conquest over death and karma.</summary>
<keywords>Raja Yoga, Samadhi, Guru, Kundalini, Prana, Sushumna, Karma, Liberation, Amaroli, Vajroli</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the fourth chapter of the Hatha Yoga Pradipika, focusing on the state of Samadhi (deep meditative absorption). The previous page established that liberation and the "natural state" (sahajavastha) are only possible through the guidance of a guru and the awakening of the internal energy (Kundalini) into the central channel.</meta>

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

->How can knowledge arise in the mind so long as the <term>life-force</term><note>original: "Prâṇa"</note> is still active and the mind has not "died"? <note>The "death" of the mind refers to the complete cessation of mental fluctuations or restless thoughts.</note> Only the person who can cause both the life-force and the mind to dissolve attains <term>liberation</term><note>original: "mokṣa"</note>; no one else can. 15.<-
<note>original: "ज्ञानं कुतो मनसि संभवतीह तावत् / प्राणोऽप्यजीयति मनो म्रियते न यावत् । / प्राणो मनो द्वयमिदं विलयं नयेद्यो- / मोक्षं स गच्छति नरो न कथंचिदन्यः ॥ १५ ॥"</note>

->Having understood the secret of the <term>central channel</term><note>original: "Suṣumnâ"</note> and having directed the <term>breath</term><note>original: "Vâyu"</note> into the middle path, one should always reside in a suitable place and restrain the breath in the <term>aperture of Brahma</term><note>original: "Brahma randhra"; the spiritual energy center at the crown of the head.</note>. 16.<-
<note>original: "ज्ञात्वा सुषुम्नासद्भेदं कृत्वा वायुं च मध्यगम् ॥ / स्थित्वा सदैव सुस्थाने ब्रह्मरन्ध्रे निरोधयेत् ॥ १६ ॥"</note>

->The sun and the moon create time in the form of day and night. That the central channel "devours" this time—effectively transcending death—is declared to be a great secret. 17.<-
<note>original: "सूर्याचंद्रमसौ धत्तः कालं रात्रिंदिवात्मकम् ॥ / भोक्त्री सुषुम्ना कालस्य गुह्यमेतदुदाहृतम् ॥ १७ ॥"</note>

->Within this "cage" of a body, there are 72,000 openings for the <term>energy channels</term><note>original: "Nâdis"</note>. Among these, the central channel is the <term>power of Shiva</term><note>original: "Śâmbhavî Śakti"</note>; all the others are of no use. 18.<-
<note>original: "द्वासप्ततिसहस्राणि नाडीद्वाराणि पंजरे ॥ / सुषुम्ना शांभवी शक्तिः शेषास्त्वेव निरर्थकाः ॥ १८ ॥"</note>

->When the breath is mastered, it awakens the <term>coiled energy</term><note>original: "Kuṇḍali"</note> through the internal fire; the practitioner should then cause the breath to enter the central channel without hindrance. 19.<-
<note>original: "वायुः परिचितो यस्मादग्निना सह कुण्डलीम् ॥ / बोधयित्वा सुषुम्नायां प्रविशेदनिरोधतः ॥ १९ ॥"</note>

->When the life-force flows through the central channel, the state of <term>mind-transcendence</term><note>original: "manonmanî"; a state where the mind is absorbed into pure consciousness and thoughts cease.</note> is surely achieved. Otherwise, all other practices are merely an exercise in exhaustion for the yogis. 20.<-
<note>original: "सुषुम्ना वाहिनि प्राणे सिद्ध्यत्येव मनोन्मनी ॥ / अन्यथा त्वितराभ्यासाः प्रयासायैव योगिनाम् ॥ २० ॥"</note>

->By whatever means the breath is restrained, the mind is also restrained; and by whatever means the mind is restrained, the breath is also restrained. 21. <note>This highlights the symbiotic relationship between respiration and thought; controlling the physical breath is the primary tool in Hatha Yoga for silencing the mind.</note><-
<note>original: "पवनो बध्यते येन मनस्तेनैव बध्यते ॥ / मनश्च बध्यते येन पवनस्तेन बध्यते ॥ २१ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page explains the vital connection between the breath (Prana) and the mind, asserting that liberation is only possible when both are stilled and directed into the central energy channel (Sushumna). It identifies the Sushumna as the only essential pathway among the body's 72,000 channels for reaching the state of mind-transcendence.</summary>
<keywords>Sushumna, Prana, Manonmani, Kundalini, Nadis, breath control, liberation, Hatha Yoga</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the fourth chapter of the Hatha Yoga Pradipika, building on the previous page's discussion of the central channel and the necessity of dissolving the mind and life-force to achieve liberation.</meta>

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

->The mind has two primary causes: <term>latent tendencies</term><note>original: "Vâsanâ"; refers to subconscious inclinations or desires left by past experiences.</note> and the <term>breath</term><note>original: "Samīraṇa" or "Prâṇa".</note>. When one of these is destroyed, both are destroyed. 22.<-
<note>original: "हेतुद्वयं तु चित्तस्य वासना च समीरतः ॥ तयोर्विनष्ट एकस्मिन्त्तौ द्वावपि विनश्यतः ॥ २२ ॥"</note>

->Where the mind dissolves, the breath also dissolves; and where the breath is absorbed, the mind also dissolves. 23.<-
<note>original: "मनो यत्र विलीयेत पवनस्तत्र लीयते ॥ पवनो लीयते यत्र मनस्तत्र विलीयेत ॥ २३ ॥"</note>

->Like milk and water mixed together, the mind and the breath are united and move in unison. Where the breath is active, the mind begins its activities; where the mind is active, the breath begins its activities. 24.<-
<note>original: "दुग्धांबुवत्संमिलितौ वुभौ तौ तुल्यक्रियौमानस मारुतौहि ॥ यतो मरुत्तत्रमनःप्रवृत्तिर्यतो मनस्तत्र मरुत्प्रवृत्तिः ॥ २४ ॥"</note>

->Therefore, through the cessation of one, the other also ceases; and by the operation of one, the other operates. As long as these two are active, the <term>senses</term><note>original: "Indriyas"</note> continue their functions. When they are stilled, the state of <term>liberation</term><note>original: "mokṣa"</note> is achieved. 25.<-
<note>original: "तत्रैकनाशादपरस्य नाश एकप्रवृत्तेरपरप्रवृत्तिः ॥ अध्वस्तयोश्चेंद्रियवर्गवृत्तिःप्रध्वस्तयोर्मोक्षपदस्य सिद्धिः ॥ २५ ॥"</note>

->By their very nature, <term>mercury</term><note>original: "Rasa"; in alchemical yoga, mercury is treated as a physical counterpart to the mind due to its "restless" or "unsteady" nature.</note> and the mind are inherently unstable. Is there anything in this world that cannot be accomplished once these two are made steady and bound? 26.<-
<note>original: "रसस्य मनसश्चैव चंचलत्वं स्वभावतः ॥ रसो बद्धो मनो बद्धं किन्न सिद्धयति भूतले ॥ २६ ॥"</note>

->O <term>Pârvati</term><note>The goddess to whom the God Shiva is explaining these secrets.</note>! Mercury and the breath—when processed and stilled—remove diseases; when "killed" (fixed), they can even restore life. When properly bound and controlled, they grant the power of <term>moving through the air</term><note>original: "Khecaratvam"; a supernatural power (siddhi) often mentioned in yogic texts.</note>. 27.<-
<note>original: "मूर्च्छितोहरते व्याधीन्मृतो जीवयति स्वयम् ॥ बद्धः खेचरतां धत्ते रसो वायुश्च पार्वति ॥ २७ ॥"</note>

->When the mind is stilled, the breath becomes steady, which in turn causes the <term>vital essence</term><note>original: "bindu"; literally "drop," referring to the body's essential energy or reproductive fluid, which yogis seek to preserve and move upward.</note> to become stable. Through the stability of this essence, <term>purity of being</term><note>original: "sattva"</note> is always maintained, leading to a firm and steady body. 28.<-
<note>original: "मनः स्थैर्यै स्थिरो वायुस्ततो बिंदुः स्थिरो भवेत् ॥ बिंदुस्थैर्यात्सदा सत्त्वं पिंडस्थैर्यं प्रजायते ॥ २८ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page explains the symbiotic relationship between the mind and the breath, asserting that controlling one inevitably controls the other. It also introduces alchemical analogies comparing the mind to mercury and describes how stabilizing these forces leads to health, supernatural powers, and spiritual liberation.</summary>
<keywords>mind-breath connection, Vâsanâ, Prâna, alchemy, Mercury, Bindu, Pârvati, liberation</keywords>

--- Page 59 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion on the relationship between the mind and breath from the fourth chapter of the Hatha Yoga Pradipika. It moves from the interdependence of breath and mind into the definition of "Laya" (absorption) and introduces the practice of Shambhavi Mudra.</meta>

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

->The mind is the master of the senses, and the <term>breath</term><note>original: "mārutaḥ" or "prāṇa"</note> is the master of the mind. The breath, in turn, is mastered by <term>absorption</term><note>original: "laya"</note>, and that absorption rests upon the <term>inner sound</term><note>original: "nâda"; the subtle internal sound heard during deep meditation.</note>. 29.<-
<note>original: "इंद्रियाणां मनो नाथो मनोनाथस्तु मारुतः ॥ मारुतस्यलयोनाथः सलयो नादमाश्रितः ॥ २९ ॥"</note>

->Let this state be called <term>liberation</term><note>original: "mokṣa"</note>, or perhaps not, according to different schools of thought; but when the mind and breath are absorbed, a certain ecstasy arises. 30.<-
<note>original: "सोऽयमेवास्तु मोक्षाख्यो मास्तु वापि मतांतरे ॥ मनः प्राणलये कश्चिदानंदः संप्रवर्तते ॥ ३० ॥"</note>

->When inhalation and exhalation have ceased and the grasping of the senses is destroyed, the Yogî attains a state of absorption that is motionless, changeless, and victorious. 31.<-
<note>original: "प्रनष्टश्वासनिःश्वासः प्रध्वस्तविषयग्रहः ॥ निश्चेष्टो निर्विकारश्च लयो जयति योगिनाम् ॥ ३१ ॥"</note>

->When all thoughts are severed and every movement has completely ceased, a state of absorption is born that can only be known through one's own experience; it is beyond the power of words to describe. 32.<-
<note>original: "उच्छिन्नसर्वसंकल्पो निःशेषाशेषचेष्टितः ॥ स्वावगम्यो लयः कोऽपि जायते वागगोचरः ॥ ३२ ॥"</note>

->People often speak of "Absorption, Absorption," but what is the true nature of this state? Absorption is simply the forgetting of the objects of the senses when <term>latent desires</term><note>original: "Vâsanâs"; subconscious inclinations or mental imprints.</note> no longer resurface. 33.<-
<note>original: "लयो लय इति प्राहुः कीदृशं लयल्क्षणम् ॥ अपुनर्वासनात्थानल्लयो विषयविस्मृतिः ॥ ३३ ॥"</note>

### The Shambhavi Mudra

->The <term>Vedas</term><note>The oldest layer of Sanskrit literature and the oldest scriptures of Hinduism.</note>, the <term>Shastras</term><note>Systematic treatises or sacred scriptures.</note>, and the <term>Puranas</term><note>Vast collections of Indian myths and lore.</note> are like ordinary public women. Only the <term>Shambhavi Mudra</term><note>A technique involving a fixed gaze and internal focus.</note> is kept secluded, like a modest lady of a noble house. 34.<-
<note>original: "वेदशास्त्रपुराणानि सामान्यगणिका इव ॥ एकैव शांभवी मुद्रा गुप्ता कुलवधूरिव ॥ ३४ ॥"</note>

->*The Shambhavi Mudra.*<-

->Focusing on the <term>Ultimate Reality</term><note>original: "Brahman"</note> within while keeping the eyes directed toward external objects without blinking; this is the Shambhavi Mudra, hidden deep within the sacred scriptures. 35.<-
<note>original: "अंतर्लक्ष्यं बहिर्दृष्टिर्निमेषोन्मेषवर्जिता ॥ एषा सा शांभवी मुद्रा वेदशास्त्रेषु गोपिता ॥ ३५ ॥"</note>

<summary>This page defines the chain of command in yoga: senses are ruled by the mind, the mind by the breath, and the breath by spiritual absorption (Laya). It concludes by introducing the Shambhavi Mudra, a secretive and highly esteemed practice of internal focus with an external gaze.</summary>
<keywords>Laya, Nada, Moksha, Shambhavi Mudra, Brahman, Vasanas, mental absorption, breath control</keywords>

--- Page 60 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general definition of absorption (Laya) to specific techniques. It details the Shambhavi Mudra and the Unmani state, focusing on how internal focus and specific physical gazes lead to the manifestation of the supreme reality (Shiva).</meta>

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

->When the Yogi remains with the mind and <term>breath</term><note>original: "pavana", also referring to prāṇa.</note> absorbed in the internal goal,<-
->with a steady gaze, looking outward and downward yet seeing nothing at all,<-
->this is truly the <term>Shambhavi Mudra</term><note>A "seal" or gesture named after Shambhu (Shiva), where the eyes are open but the attention is directed entirely inward.</note>, attained only through the grace of a <term>Guru</term><note>A spiritual teacher.</note>.<-
->In that state, the supreme reality of Shambhu manifests—a state beyond both the void and the non-void. 36.<-

When the Yogî remains inwardly attentive to the <term>Brahman</term><note>The ultimate, infinite reality.</note>, keeping the mind and the <term>Prâṇa</term><note>Life-force or breath.</note> absorbed, and the sight steady, as if seeing everything while in reality seeing nothing outside, below, or above, verily then it is called the Sâmbhavî Mudrâ, which is learnt by the favour of a teacher. Whatever wonderful state, whether characterized by emptiness or non-emptiness, is perceived, it is to be regarded as the manifestation of that great <term>Śambhû</term><note>A name for Shiva, representing the auspicious state of pure consciousness.</note>. 36.

->From the different seats of the auspicious Shambhavi and <term>Khechari</term><note>The "sky-moving" gesture where the tongue is turned back into the nasal cavity.</note> mudras,<-
->the bliss of mental absorption arises in the <term>Void</term><note>original: "śūnya"; a state of consciousness free from objective thought.</note>, which consists of the light of joy. 37.<-

The two states, the Sâmbhavî and the Khecharî, are different because of their seats (being the heart and the space between the eyebrows respectively); but both cause happiness, for the mind becomes absorbed in the <term>Self of Joy and Consciousness</term><note>original: "Chita-sukha-Rupa-âtmana"</note> which is the void. 37.

### Now, the Unmani

->Fixing the gaze on the light and slightly raising the eyebrows,<-
->while uniting the mind as in the previous practice, the state of <term>Unmani</term><note>literally "mindless" or "beyond mind"; a state of thoughtless awareness.</note> is achieved in an instant. 38.<-

->*The Unmanî.*<-

Fix the gaze on the light (seen on the tip of the nose) and raise the eyebrows a little, with the mind contemplating as before (in the Śâmbhavî Mudrâ, that is, inwardly thinking of the absolute, but apparently looking outside.) This will create the Unmanî state at once. 38.

->Some are lost in the web of the <term>Agamas</term><note>Traditional tantric scriptures.</note>, some in the complexities of the <term>Nigamas</term><note>The Vedas or orthodox scriptures.</note>,<-
->and others are bewildered by logic; none truly know the <term>Taraka</term><note>The "Deliverer"; a specific yogic focus on light that carries the practitioner across the ocean of worldly existence.</note>. 39.<-

->*The Târaka.*<-

Some are devoted to the Vedas, some to the Nigama, while others are enwrapt in Logic, but none knows the value of this mudrâ, which enables one to cross the ocean of existence. 39.

->With half-closed eyes, a steady mind, and the gaze fixed on the tip of the nose,<-
->dissolving both the <term>Moon</term><note>original: "candra"; the Ida breath channel on the left.</note> and the <term>Sun</term><note>original: "arka"; the Pingala breath channel on the right.</note> into a state of complete motionlessness;<-
->he who reaches that form of light, the seed of all, the radiant and supreme<-
->Reality, he attains that State; what more can be said here? 40.<-

With steady calm mind and half-closed eyes, fixed on the tip of the nose, stopping the Idâ and the Pingalâ without blinking, he who...

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

...can see the light which is the all, the seed, the entire brilliant, great <term>Truth</term><note>original: "Tatwama" (Tattva), referring to the essence or reality.</note>, and approaches Him, who is the great object. What is the use of more talk? 40.

->One should not worship the <term>Linga</term><note>The symbol of Shiva, representing the Ātman or Supreme Soul.</note> during the day, nor should one worship it at night.<-
->One should always worship the Linga by restraining both day and night. 41.<-

<note>In yogic symbolism, "day" refers to the flow of breath through the right channel (Pingalā) and "night" refers to the flow through the left channel (Iḍā). Restraining both means directing the breath into the central channel (Suṣumṇā).</note>

One should not meditate on the <term>Linga</term> (the <term>Soul</term><note>original: "Ātman"</note>) in the day (while the <term>Sun</term><note>original: "Sûrya"</note> or <term>Pingalâ</term> channel is active) or at night (when the <term>Idâ</term> channel is active), but should always contemplate after restraining both. 41.

### Now, the <term>Khecharî</term><note>Literally "moving in the sky (consciousness)." A mudrā where the tongue is turned back into the nasal cavity.</note>

->When the <term>breath</term><note>original: "māruta", the vital wind.</note> situated in the left and right channels moves into the middle,<-
->the Khecharî Mudrâ is established in that place; of this, there is no doubt. 42.<-

->*The Khecharî.*<-

When the air has ceased to move in the right and the left nostrils, and has begun to flow in the middle path, then the Khecharî Mudrâ can be accomplished there. There is no doubt of this. 42.

->One should swallow the <term>breath</term><note>original: "anila"</note> into the <term>Void</term><note>original: "śūnya", referring here to the Suṣumṇā or central channel.</note> between the Idâ and the Pingalâ.<-
->There, the Khecharî Mudrâ truly becomes steady, again and again. 43.<-

If the <term>Life-force</term><note>original: "Prâna"</note> can be drawn into the Void (<term>Susumnâ</term>), which is between the Idâ and the Pingalâ, and made motionless there, then the Khecharî Mudrâ can truly become steady there. 43.

->That Mudrâ is called Khecharî which is performed in the supportless space between the <term>Sun</term> and the <term>Moon</term><note>original: "Sūrya" and "Chandra", the right and left channels.</note><-
->and is situated in the <term>Sky-Circle</term><note>original: "Vyoma Chakra", a center of energy in the upper part of the head.</note>. 44.<-

That Mudrâ is called Khecharî which is performed in the supportless space between the <term>Sun</term> and the <term>Moon</term> (the Idâ and the Pingalâ) and called the <term>Vyoma Chakra</term>. 44.

->The stream that rises from the <term>Moon</term><note>original: "Soma", the source of divine nectar in the head.</note> is truly the beloved of <term>Shiva</term>.<-
->One should fill the incomparable divine <term>Susumnâ</term> at its rear opening. 45.<-

The Khecharî which causes the stream to flow from the <term>Moon</term> is beloved of <term>Siva</term>. The incomparable divine Susumnâ should be closed by the tongue drawn back <note>The "rear opening" or "western mouth" refers to the upper cavity reached by the tongue.</note>. 45.

->It can also be filled from the front, and then surely it becomes the Khecharî.<-
->By practice, the Khecharî Mudrâ leads to the <term>Mindless State</term><note>original: "Unmanî", a state of high consciousness beyond thought.</note>. 46.<-

It can be closed from the front also (by stopping the movements of the Prâna), and then surely it becomes the Khecharî. By practice, this Khecharî leads to Unmanî. 46.

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

->The seat of <term>Shiva</term><note>The auspicious Lord, representing the highest state of consciousness.</note> is between the eyebrows, and the mind becomes absorbed there. This state is known as the <term>Fourth State</term><note>original: "Tûrya" (Turiya), the state of pure consciousness that exists beyond waking, dreaming, and deep sleep.</note>, and <term>Death</term><note>original: "Kâlo", which means both "time" and "death."</note> has no power there. 47.<-

->One should practice the <term>Sky-moving Seal</term><note>original: "Khecharî Mudrâ."</note> until <term>Yogic Sleep</term><note>original: "Yoga-nidrâ", a state of conscious deep sleep or profound meditative absorption (Samâdhi).</note> is attained. For one who has reached this state of Yogic Sleep, death never comes. 48.<-

->Freeing the mind from all thoughts and thinking of nothing, one should sit firmly like a jar in <term>space</term><note>original: "Vyomni" (Akasha), the ether or subtle space.</note>, filled both inside and out. 49.<-

->Just as the external air becomes still, the internal breath undoubtedly does the same. The breath, along with the mind, reaches stability in its proper place <note>The "proper place" refers to the <term>Opening of Brahman</term> (Brahma-randhra), the energy center at the crown of the head.</note>. 50.<-

->For one practicing this way in the path of the breath day and night, the breath is mastered through practice, and the mind dissolves in that very place. 51.<-

->By bathing the body from head to toe with the <term>Nectar of Immortality</term><note>original: "Amrita", a divine fluid believed to flow from the "moon" center in the head during advanced yoga.</note>, one gains a <term>Great Body</term><note>original: "Mahâkâyâ", a perfected physical form.</note> of immense strength and courage. 52.<-
->*End of the instructions on the Sky-moving Seal.*<-

->By placing the mind within the <term>Primal Power</term><note>original: "Shakti", here referring to the Kundalini energy.</note> and bringing that Power into the center of the mind, and by observing the mind with the mind, the <term>Supreme State</term><note>original: "Param Pada" or "Brahma", the ultimate reality.</note> should be obtained. 53.<-

->Place the <term>Self</term><note>original: "Atman"</note> in the center of <term>Space</term><note>original: "Kha", the void or ether.</note>, and place Space in the center of the Self. Making everything consist of that Space, one should think of nothing at all. 54.<-

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for meditative absorption following the practice of the Khechari Mudra. It moves from physical techniques into the psychological and philosophical dissolution of the mind into the supreme reality.</meta>

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

Keep the <term>Self</term><note>original: "âtmâ"</note> inside the <term>Infinite</term><note>original: "Kha", often signifying the void or the space of the heart/Brahma.</note> and place the <term>Absolute</term><note>original: "Brahma"</note> inside your Self. Having made everything pervaded with the Absolute, think of nothing else. 54.

->Empty within, empty without, like a jar in the open sky.<-
->Full within, full without, like a jar submerged in the ocean. 55.<-
<note>This imagery illustrates the state of Samadhi: the yogi is "empty" of individual ego and worldly thoughts, yet "full" of universal consciousness.</note>

->One should not think of the outside world, nor of the inner world.<-
->Abandoning all concerns, one should think of nothing at all. 56.<-

->The entirety of this world is but a construction of thought.<-
->The play of the mind is nothing more than the activity of thought.<-
->Discarding these mental projections, O Rama, take refuge in the state beyond thought, and attain the certainty of peace. 57.<-
<note>Addressing "Rama" is a traditional literary device, likely referencing the teachings found in the *Yoga Vashistha*, where the sage Vashistha instructs Prince Rama on the illusory nature of the world.</note>

->Just as camphor vanishes in fire, and rock salt dissolves in water,<-
->so the mind, when united with the ultimate Truth, merges and loses its separate identity. 58.<-

->Everything that can be known is perceived, and the mind itself is called "knowledge."<-
->When both the knowledge and the object of perception are equally dissolved, there is no second path—Duality is destroyed. 59.<-

->Everything in this world, whether moving or still, is an object of the mind.<-
->When the mind reaches the <term>transcendental state</term><note>original: "unmanî avasthâ", a state of "mindlessness" or "supra-mental" consciousness where the mind is no longer active.</note>, <term>duality</term><note>original: "dwaita"</note> is no longer perceived because the mind has ceased its functions. 60.<-

->By abandoning all objects of knowledge, the mind reaches dissolution.<-
->Once the mind has dissolved, only <term>Absolute Liberation</term><note>original: "Kaivalya", the state of pure, solitary existence or ultimate spiritual independence.</note> remains. 61.<-

<summary>This page describes the process of mental dissolution (Laya), using metaphors like salt in water to explain how the individual mind merges into the Absolute. It emphasizes that the perceived world is a product of thought and that liberation (Kaivalya) is achieved when both the mind and its objects of perception vanish.</summary>

<keywords>Samadhi, dissolution of mind, Atma, Brahma, Unmani Avastha, Duality, Kaivalya, Yoga Vashistha</keywords>

### THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

->Thus, various methods, verified by personal experience,<-
->the paths to <term>meditative absorption</term><note>original: "Samadhi"</note> have been described by the great-souled <term>Teachers</term><note>original: "Âchâryas"</note> of old. 62.<-

The high-souled Teachers of yore gained experience in the various methods of absorption themselves, and then they preached them to others. 62.

->Salutations to you, O <term>Central Channel</term><note>original: "Susumnâ", the primary energetic conduit in the spine.</note>, to you O <term>Serpent Power</term><note>original: "Kundalinî"</note>, to you O <term>Nectar</term><note>original: "Sudhâ"</note> born of the Moon,<-
->to you O <term>State Beyond Mind</term><note>original: "Manonmanî"</note>, to you O Great Power, Energy, and the Intelligent Spirit! 63.<-

Salutations to Thee, O Susumnâ, to Thee O Kundalinî, to Thee O Sudhâ, born of the Moon <note>original: "Chandra"</note>, to Thee O Manonmanî! to Thee O great power, energy and the intelligent spirit. 63.

->Even for those unable to grasp the true nature of reality, and even for the simple-minded,<-
->the practice of <term>Internal Sound</term><note>original: "nâda"</note> has been taught by <term>Goraksha Natha</term><note>A famous 10th-century sage and founding master of the Hatha Yoga tradition.</note>. 64.<-

I will describe now the practice of the <term>unstruck sound</term><note>original: "anâhata nâda", a sound not caused by two objects hitting each other, but heard internally during meditation.</note>, as propounded by Goraksha Natha, for the benefit of those who are unable to understand the principles of knowledge—a method which is liked by the ignorant also. 64.

->The <term>Primal Lord</term><note>original: "Âdinâtha", referring to Shiva.</note> taught one and a quarter <term>crore</term><note>A "crore" is ten million. This signifies 12.5 million distinct methods of meditation.</note> ways to attain <term>absorption</term><note>original: "laya"</note>.<-
->Among all these, we consider the concentration on Internal Sound to be the most important. 65.<-

Âdinâtha propounded 12.5 million methods of trance, and they are all extant. Of these, the hearing of the unstruck sound is the only one, the chief, in my opinion. 65.

->Sitting in the <term>Pose of Liberation</term><note>original: "Mukta Âsana", a seated meditative posture.</note> and adopting the <term>Gesture of Shiva</term><note>original: "Śâmbhavî Mudrâ", a practice of gazing at the "third eye" point while keeping the mind still.</note>,<-
->the Yogi should listen with a focused mind to the sound inside his right ear. 66.<-

Sitting with Mukta Âsana and with the Śâmbhavî Mudrâ, the Yogî should hear the sound inside his right ear, with a collected mind. 66.

->The ears, the eyes, the nose, and the mouth should be closed.<-
->Then, in the purified path of the <term>Central Channel</term><note>original: "Suṣumṇâ"</note>, a clear and stainless sound is heard. 67.<-

The ears, the eyes, the nose, and the mouth should be closed and then the clear sound is heard in the passage of the Suṣumṇâ which has been cleansed of all its impurities. 67.

->The Beginning, the Jar,<-
->the Familiarity, and the Completion—<-
->these are the four stages found in all systems of Yoga. 68.<-

In all the Yogas, there are four states: (1) <term>Beginning</term><note>original: "ârambha"</note>, (2) <term>The Jar</term><note>original: "Ghata", symbolizing the body becoming a perfected vessel.</note>, (3) <term>Familiarity</term><note>original: "Parichaya"</note>, (4) <term>Completion</term><note>original: "niṣpatti", or the state of consummation.</note>. 68.

It describes the physical technique of blocking the senses to hear internal vibrations and outlines the four progressive stages of yogic mastery.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the detailed explanation of "Nada Yoga" or the Yoga of Internal Sound. Following the introduction of the lineage on the previous page, this section outlines the first three of four technical stages of spiritual progress—the Beginning, Vessel, and Accumulation stages—identifying the specific internal sounds and physical changes that accompany each.</meta>

# THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.
I
<meta>Institutional stamps are visible at the top left and right corners of the original document.</meta>

### The Beginning Stage
### When the Knot of Brahma is pierced, a state of bliss arises from the void;
### various tinkling sounds are heard within the body, like the "Unstruck Sound." 69.

->*The Beginning Stage*<-
<note>original: "Ârambha Avasthâ"</note>

When the <term>Knot of Brahma</term><note>original: "Brahma granthi", a spiritual or energetic block located in the region of the heart.</note> is pierced through the practice of <term>breath control</term><note>original: "Prânâyâma"</note>, a sense of happiness is experienced within the empty space of the heart. At this time, the <term>Internal Sounds</term><note>original: "anâhat sounds"; literally "unstruck" because they are not caused by physical contact.</note>, resembling the delicate tinkling of jewelry, are heard vibrating within the practitioner's body. 69.

### With a divine body, radiant and fragrant, free from all disease,
### with a heart filled with the void, the practitioner enters the Beginning Stage. 70.

In the <term>Beginning Stage</term><note>original: "ârambha"</note>, the practitioner’s body becomes divine, glowing, and healthy, emitting a celestial fragrance. The heart becomes entirely still, as if it were a vast void. 70.

### The Vessel Stage
### In the second stage, the breaths are united and enter the middle path;
### the practitioner becomes firm in posture and wise like a god. 71.

->*The Vessel Stage*<-
<note>original: "Ghata Avasthâ"</note>

In the second stage, the internal energies are unified and begin to flow through the <term>Central Channel</term><note>original: "madhyaga"; referring to the Sushumna, the primary energy conduit in the spine.</note>. The practitioner’s posture becomes perfectly stable, and they attain the wisdom of a divine being. 71.

### By piercing the Knot of Vishnu, the sign of supreme bliss appears;
### then, in the "great void" of the throat, the sound of a kettle-drum resonates. 72.

By this practice, the <term>Knot of Vishnu</term><note>original: "Vishnu granthi", a spiritual block located in the region of the throat.</note> is pierced, which is signaled by the experience of highest pleasure. Then, a sound like a <term>kettle-drum</term><note>original: "Bheri sound"</note> is produced in the void of the throat. 72.

### The Stage of Accumulation
### In the third stage, the sound of a drum is known to arise in the sky;
### the breath then reaches the Great Void, the source of all powers. 73.

->*The Stage of Accumulation*<-
<note>original: "Parichaya Avasthâ"</note>

In the third stage, a sound like that of a drum is heard arising in the space between the eyebrows. The <term>breath</term><note>original: "Vâyu"</note> then reaches the <term>Great Void</term><note>original: "Mahâsûnya"</note>, which is the source of all <term>supernatural powers</term><note>original: "siddhis"</note>. 73.

### Having then transcended the pleasures of the mind, spontaneous bliss arises;
### free from impurity, pain, aging, disease, hunger, and sleep. 74.

Once the practitioner overcomes the common pleasures of the mind, a state of <term>spontaneous bliss</term><note>original: "sahajânanda"</note> is born. This state is free from all defects, suffering, old age, disease, hunger, and the need for sleep. 74.

<summary>This page details the first three stages of yogic advancement (Arambha, Ghata, and Parichaya), describing how breath control leads to the "piercing" of spiritual knots in the heart and throat. It correlates each stage with specific internal sounds, such as tinkling ornaments and drums, as well as physical and mental purification.</summary>
<keywords>Arambha Avastha, Ghata Avastha, Parichaya Avastha, Brahma granthi, Vishnu granthi, internal sound, anahata nada, breath control, spiritual progress, Sushumna</keywords>

# THE HATHA YOGA PRADIPIKA.

<meta>Institutional stamps are visible at the top left and right corners of the original document.</meta>

->When the <term>Knot of Rudra</term><note>original: "Rudra granthi"; the final psychic energy block located in the area of the forehead or Third Eye.</note> is pierced and the breath reaches the seat of the Lord, then in the <term>Completion Stage</term><note>original: "Nishpatti"; the fourth and final stage of success in this practice.</note>, a sound like a flute or a resonating lute is produced. 75.<-

When the <term>Knot of Rudra</term> is pierced and the air enters the seat of the Lord (the space between the eyebrows), then the perfect sound like that of a flute is produced. 75.

->At that time, the mind becomes one with the sound; this is called <term>Royal Yoga</term><note>original: "Râja-Yoga"; the highest form of yoga focusing on the mind and consciousness, to which the physical Hatha Yoga is a preparation.</note>. The great Yogi becomes equal to the Lord of the Universe, acting as a creator and destroyer. 76.<-

The union of the mind and the sound is called the <term>Royal Yoga</term>. The (real) <term>Yogî</term> becomes the creator and destroyer of the universe, like God. 76.

->Whether <term>liberation</term><note>original: "mukti"</note> is achieved or not, there is unbroken happiness here. This bliss, arising from <term>absorption</term><note>original: "Laya"; the state where the mind dissolves into the object of meditation.</note>, is obtained through Royal Yoga. 77.<-

Perpetual Happiness is achieved by this; I do not care if the <term>liberation</term> be not attained. This happiness, resulting from absorption [in the Absolute], is obtained by means of <term>Royal Yoga</term>. 77.

->Those who are ignorant of Royal Yoga and practice only the physical techniques of <term>Hatha Yoga</term><note>original: "Haṭha-Yoga"; the yoga of physical effort, postures, and breath control.</note>, I consider these practitioners to be wasting their energy without results. 78.<-

Those who are ignorant of the <term>Royal Yoga</term> and practise only the <term>Haṭha-Yoga</term>, will, in my opinion, waste their energy fruitlessly. 78.

->To quickly reach the <term>mindless state</term><note>original: "Unmanî"; a state of high consciousness where the habitual mind ceases to fluctuate.</note>, I recommend meditation on the space between the eyebrows. This is an easy method for those of small intellect to reach the state of Royal Yoga. The absorption born of the <term>internal sound</term><note>original: "nâda"</note> immediately grants spiritual experience. 79.<-

Contemplation on the space between the eyebrows is, in my opinion, best for accomplishing soon the *mindless* state. For people of small intellect, it is a very easy method for obtaining perfection in the <term>Royal Yoga</term>. The *absorption* produced by the *internal sound*, at once gives experience (of spiritual powers). 79.

->The unique bliss that grows in the hearts of the Great Yogis who have attained <term>deep meditation</term><note>original: "Samâdhi"</note> through focus on the internal sound is beyond words; it is known only to the holy <term>Lord Guru</term><note>original: "Śri Gurû Nâtha"</note>. 80.<-

The happiness which increases in the hearts of the Great Yogis, who have gained success in <term>deep meditation</term> by means of attention to the *internal sound*, is beyond description, and is known to the *Holy Master* alone. 80.

->Closing the ears with both hands, the sage hears a sound. He should fix his mind steadily on that sound until he reaches the state of absolute stillness. 81.<-
->As this internal sound is practiced, it eventually drowns out all external noises. The sage should fix his mind there until he reaches the state of absolute stillness. 81.<-

<note>This verse 81 appears twice in the original transcription with slight variations in the Sanskrit, emphasizing the technique of blocking out the physical world to hear the internal vibration.</note>

<summary>This page describes the final "Completion Stage" of yoga, where the practitioner hears flute-like sounds and achieves a god-like state of bliss through Royal Yoga. It warns that physical yoga alone, without this mental absorption, is a fruitless waste of effort.</summary>
<keywords>Completion Stage (Nishpatti), Knot of Rudra (Rudra granthi), Royal Yoga (Raja Yoga), Internal Sound (Nada), Mindless State (Unmani), Absorption (Laya), Deep Meditation (Samadhi)</keywords>
